   284→diff --git a/desloppify/languages/javascript/phases_nextjs.py b/desloppify/languages/javascript/phases_nextjs.py
   285→new file mode 100644
   286→index 00000000..95d26834
   287→--- /dev/null
   288→+++ b/desloppify/languages/javascript/phases_nextjs.py
   289→@@ -0,0 +1,36 @@
   290→+"""Next.js framework smells for JavaScript/JSX projects.
   291→+
   292→+This phase intentionally reuses the shared (data-driven) framework detector and
   293→+Next.js smell scanners used by the TypeScript plugin so JS-only repos get the
   294→+same depth.
   295→+"""
   296→+
   297→+from __future__ import annotations
   298→+
   299→+from pathlib import Path
   300→+
   301→+from desloppify.base.output.terminal import log
   302→+from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import LangRuntimeContract
   303→+from desloppify.languages.typescript.frameworks import detect_primary_ts_framework
   304→+from desloppify.languages.typescript.frameworks.nextjs import nextjs_info_from_detection
   305→+from desloppify.languages.typescript.frameworks.nextjs.phase import (
   306→+    detect_nextjs_framework_smells,
   307→+)
   308→+from desloppify.state_io import Issue
   309→+
   310→+
   311→+def phase_nextjs(
   312→+    path: Path,
   313→+    lang: LangRuntimeContract,
   314→+) -> tuple[list[Issue], dict[str, int]]:
   315→+    framework = detect_primary_ts_framework(path, lang)
   316→+    nextjs_info = nextjs_info_from_detection(framework)
   317→+    if not nextjs_info.is_primary:
   318→+        return [], {}
   319→+
   320→+    scan_root = nextjs_info.package_root
   321→+    return detect_nextjs_framework_smells(scan_root, nextjs_info, log)
   322→+
   323→+
   324→+__all__ = ["phase_nextjs"]
   325→+

   332→+"""Tests for JavaScript Next.js framework smells integration."""
   333→+
   334→+from __future__ import annotations
   335→+
   336→+from pathlib import Path
   337→+
   338→+import pytest
   339→+
   340→+import desloppify.languages.javascript  # noqa: F401 (registration side effect)
   341→+from desloppify.languages.framework import get_lang
   342→+from desloppify.languages.javascript.phases_nextjs import phase_nextjs
   343→+
   344→+
   345→+@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
   346→+def _root(tmp_path, set_project_root):
   347→+    """Point PROJECT_ROOT at the tmp directory via RuntimeContext."""
   348→+
   349→+
   350→+def _write(tmp_path: Path, name: str, content: str) -> Path:
   351→+    p = tmp_path / name
   352→+    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   353→+    p.write_text(content)
   354→+    return p
   355→+
   356→+
   357→+class _FakeLang:
   358→+    zone_map = None
   359→+
   360→+    def __init__(self):
   361→+        self.review_cache = {}
   362→+
   363→+
   364→+def test_javascript_plugin_includes_nextjs_phase():
   365→+    cfg = get_lang("javascript")
   366→+    labels = [getattr(p, "label", "") for p in cfg.phases]
   367→+    assert "Next.js framework smells" in labels
   368→+
   369→+
   370→+def test_phase_nextjs_emits_smells_when_nextjs_is_primary(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch):
   371→+    _write(
   372→+        tmp_path,
   373→+        "package.json",
   374→+        '{"dependencies": {"next": "14.0.0", "react": "18.3.0"}}\n',
   375→+    )
   376→+    _write(
   377→+        tmp_path,
   378→+        "app/server-in-client.jsx",
   379→+        "'use client'\nimport fs from 'node:fs'\nexport default function X(){return null}\n",
   380→+    )
   381→+
   382→+    from desloppify.languages.typescript.frameworks.nextjs import phase as next_phase_mod
   383→+
   384→+    monkeypatch.setattr(next_phase_mod, "run_next_lint", lambda _root: ([], 0, "tests disabled"))
   385→+
   386→+    issues, potentials = phase_nextjs(tmp_path, _FakeLang())
   387→+    detectors = {issue["detector"] for issue in issues}
   388→+    assert "nextjs" in detectors
   389→+    assert "next_lint" in detectors
   390→+    assert potentials.get("nextjs", 0) >= 1
   391→+    assert potentials.get("next_lint", 0) >= 1
   392→+

   459→+"""
   460→+

   462→+

   464→+
   465→+from .types import FrameworkId
   466→+
   467→+
   468→+@dataclass(frozen=True)
   469→+class FrameworkSignature:
   470→+    id: FrameworkId
   471→+    label: str
   472→+    dependencies: tuple[str, ...] = ()
   473→+    dev_dependencies: tuple[str, ...] = ()
   474→+    config_files: tuple[str, ...] = ()
   475→+    marker_files: tuple[str, ...] = ()
   476→+    marker_dirs: tuple[str, ...] = ()
   477→+
   478→+    weight_dependency: int = 3
   479→+    weight_config: int = 2
   480→+    weight_marker: int = 2
   481→+    weight_script: int = 1
   482→+
   483→+    primary_score_threshold: int = 5
   484→+
   485→+
   486→+TS_FRAMEWORK_SIGNATURES: tuple[FrameworkSignature, ...] = (
   487→+    FrameworkSignature(
   488→+        id="nextjs",
   489→+        label="Next.js",
   490→+        dependencies=("next",),
   491→+        config_files=("next.config.js", "next.config.mjs", "next.config.cjs", "next.config.ts"),
   492→+        marker_files=("next-env.d.ts",),
   493→+        marker_dirs=("app", "src/app", "pages", "src/pages"),
   494→+        primary_score_threshold=5,
   495→+    ),
   496→+    FrameworkSignature(
   497→+        id="vite",
   498→+        label="Vite",
   499→+        dependencies=("vite",),
   500→+        dev_dependencies=("@vitejs/plugin-react", "@vitejs/plugin-react-swc", "@vitejs/plugin-vue"),
   501→+        config_files=("vite.config.ts", "vite.config.js", "vite.config.mjs", "vite.config.cjs"),
   502→+        marker_files=("vite-env.d.ts", "src/vite-env.d.ts"),
   503→+        marker_dirs=("src",),
   504→+        primary_score_threshold=5,
   505→+    ),
   506→+)
   507→+

   517→+"""Generic framework detection for TypeScript projects."""
   518→+
   519→+from __future__ import annotations
   520→+
   521→+import json
   522→+import re
   523→+from pathlib import Path
   524→+from typing import Any
   525→+
   526→+from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
   527→+from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import LangRuntimeContract
   528→+
   529→+from .catalog import TS_FRAMEWORK_SIGNATURES, FrameworkSignature
   530→+from .types import FrameworkCandidate, FrameworkId, PrimaryFrameworkDetection
   531→+
   532→+[REDACTED]
   533→+
   534→+
   535→+def _find_nearest_package_json(scan_path: Path, project_root: Path) -> Path | None:
   536→+    resolved = scan_path if scan_path.is_absolute() else (project_root / scan_path)
   537→+    resolved = resolved.resolve()
   538→+    if resolved.is_file():
   539→+        resolved = resolved.parent
   540→+
   541→+    # If scan_path is inside runtime project root, cap traversal there.
   542→+    # Otherwise (e.g. --path /tmp/other-repo), traverse from scan_path upward.
   543→+    limit_to_project_root = False
   544→+    try:
   545→+        resolved.relative_to(project_root)
   546→+        limit_to_project_root = True
   547→+    except ValueError:
   548→+        limit_to_project_root = False
   549→+
   550→+    cur = resolved
   551→+    while True:
   552→+        candidate = cur / "package.json"
   553→+        if candidate.is_file():
   554→+            return candidate
   555→+        if (limit_to_project_root and cur == project_root) or cur.parent == cur:
   556→+            break
   557→+        cur = cur.parent
   558→+
   559→+    # Fallback only when no package.json exists in the scanned tree.
   560→+    candidate = project_root / "package.json"
   561→+    return candidate if candidate.is_file() else None
   562→+
   563→+
   564→+def _read_package_json(package_json: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
   565→+    try:
   566→+        payload = json.loads(package_json.read_text())
   567→+    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError, json.JSONDecodeError):
   568→+        return {}
   569→+    return payload if isinstance(payload, dict) else {}
   570→+
   571→+
   572→+def _dep_set(payload: dict[str, Any], key: str) -> set[str]:
   573→+    deps = payload.get(key)
   574→+    if not isinstance(deps, dict):
   575→+        return set()
   576→+    return {str(k) for k in deps.keys()}
   577→+
   578→+
   579→+def _script_values(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
   580→+    scripts = payload.get("scripts")
   581→+    if not isinstance(scripts, dict):
   582→+        return []
   583→+    return [v for v in scripts.values() if isinstance(v, str)]
   584→+
   585→+
   586→+def _existing_relpaths(
   587→+    package_root: Path,
   588→+    project_root: Path,
   589→+    candidates: tuple[str, ...],
   590→+    *,
   591→+    kind: str,
   592→+) -> tuple[str, ...]:
   593→+    hits: list[str] = []
   594→+    for relpath in candidates:
   595→+        path = (package_root / relpath).resolve()
   596→+        ok = path.is_dir() if kind == "dir" else path.is_file()
   597→+        if not ok:
   598→+            continue
   599→+        try:
   600→+            hits.append(path.relative_to(project_root).as_posix())
   601→+        except ValueError:
   602→+            hits.append(path.as_posix())
   603→+    return tuple(hits)
   604→+
   605→+
   606→+def _score_signature(
   607→+    sig: FrameworkSignature,
   608→+    *,
   609→+    package_root: Path,
   610→+    project_root: Path,
   611→+    deps: set[str],
   612→+    dev_deps: set[str],
   613→+    scripts: list[str],
   614→+) -> FrameworkCandidate:
   615→+    dep_hits = tuple(sorted(set(sig.dependencies).intersection(deps)))
   616→+    dev_dep_hits = tuple(sorted(set(sig.dev_dependencies).intersection(dev_deps)))
   617→+    config_hits = _existing_relpaths(package_root, project_root, sig.config_files, kind="file")
   618→+    marker_file_hits = _existing_relpaths(package_root, project_root, sig.marker_files, kind="file")
   619→+    marker_dir_hits = _existing_relpaths(package_root, project_root, sig.marker_dirs, kind="dir")
   620→+
   621→+    score = 0
   622→+    score += sig.weight_dependency if (dep_hits or dev_dep_hits) else 0
   623→+    score += sig.weight_config if config_hits else 0
   624→+    score += sig.weight_marker if (marker_file_hits or marker_dir_hits) else 0
   625→+
   626→+    script_hits: list[str] = []
   627→+    if scripts:
   628→+        if sig.id == "nextjs":
   629→+            pat = re.compile(r"(?:^|\s)next(?:\s|$)")
   630→+        elif sig.id == "vite":
   631→+            pat = re.compile(r"(?:^|\s)vite(?:\s|$)")
   632→+        else:
   633→+            pat = None
   634→+        if pat is not None:
   635→+            script_hits = [s for s in scripts if pat.search(s)]
   636→+            if script_hits:
   637→+                score += sig.weight_script
   638→+
   639→+    evidence: dict[str, Any] = {
   640→+        "dep_hits": list(dep_hits),
   641→+        "dev_dep_hits": list(dev_dep_hits),
   642→+        "config_hits": list(config_hits),
   643→+        "marker_file_hits": list(marker_file_hits),
   644→+        "marker_dir_hits": list(marker_dir_hits),
   645→+        "script_hits": script_hits[:5],
   646→+    }
   647→+
   648→+    if sig.id == "nextjs":
   649→+        evidence["app_roots"] = [
   650→+            p for p in marker_dir_hits if p.endswith("/app") or p == "app"
   651→+        ]
   652→+        evidence["pages_roots"] = [
   653→+            p for p in marker_dir_hits if p.endswith("/pages") or p == "pages"
   654→+        ]
   655→+
   656→+    return FrameworkCandidate(
   657→+        id=sig.id,
   658→+        label=sig.label,
   659→+        score=score,
   660→+        evidence=evidence,
   661→+    )
   662→+
   663→+
   664→+def detect_primary_ts_framework(
   665→+    scan_path: Path,
   666→+    lang: LangRuntimeContract | None = None,
   667→+    *,
   668→+    signatures: tuple[FrameworkSignature, ...] = TS_FRAMEWORK_SIGNATURES,
   669→+) -> PrimaryFrameworkDetection:
   670→+    """Detect the primary TS framework for this scan path (cached per run)."""
   671→+    cache_key = f"{_CACHE_KEY}:{Path(scan_path).resolve().as_posix()}"
   672→+    if lang is not None:
   673→+        cache = getattr(lang, "review_cache", None)
   674→+        if isinstance(cache, dict):
   675→+            cached = cache.get(cache_key)
   676→+            if isinstance(cached, PrimaryFrameworkDetection):
   677→+                return cached
   678→+
   679→+    project_root = get_project_root()
   680→+    package_json = _find_nearest_package_json(scan_path, project_root)
   681→+    package_root = (package_json.parent if package_json else project_root).resolve()
   682→+    payload = _read_package_json(package_json) if package_json else {}
   683→+
   684→+    deps = _dep_set(payload, "dependencies") | _dep_set(payload, "peerDependencies") | _dep_set(
   685→+        payload, "optionalDependencies"
   686→+    )
   687→+    dev_deps = _dep_set(payload, "devDependencies")
   688→+    scripts = _script_values(payload)
   689→+
   690→+    candidates = tuple(
   691→+        _score_signature(
   692→+            sig,
   693→+            package_root=package_root,
   694→+            project_root=project_root,
   695→+            deps=deps,
   696→+            dev_deps=dev_deps,
   697→+            scripts=scripts,
   698→+        )
   699→+        for sig in signatures
   700→+    )
   701→+    ordered = sorted(candidates, key=lambda c: (-c.score, c.label))
   702→+
   703→+    primary_id: FrameworkId | None = None
   704→+    primary_score = 0
   705→+    if ordered and ordered[0].score > 0:
   706→+        sig = next((s for s in signatures if s.id == ordered[0].id), None)
   707→+        threshold = sig.primary_score_threshold if sig is not None else 5
   708→+        if ordered[0].score >= threshold:
   709→+            primary_id = ordered[0].id
   710→+            primary_score = ordered[0].score
   711→+
   712→+    result = PrimaryFrameworkDetection(
   713→+        package_root=package_root,
   714→+        package_json_relpath=(
   715→+            (
   716→+                package_json.relative_to(project_root).as_posix()
   717→+                if package_json and package_json.is_relative_to(project_root)
   718→+                else package_json.as_posix()
   719→+            )
   720→+            if package_json
   721→+            else None
   722→+        ),
   723→+        primary_id=primary_id,
   724→+        primary_score=primary_score,
   725→+        candidates=tuple(ordered),
   726→+    )
   727→+
   728→+    if lang is not None:
   729→+        cache = getattr(lang, "review_cache", None)
   730→+        if isinstance(cache, dict):
   731→+            cache[cache_key] = result
   732→+
   733→+    return result
   734→+
   735→+
   736→+__all__ = ["detect_primary_ts_framework"]

   742→@@ -0,0 +1,158 @@
   743→+# Next.js Framework Module
   744→+
   745→+This document explains the Next.js framework support used by Desloppify's TypeScript and JavaScript plugins.
   746→+
   747→+It covers:
   748→+
   749→+- What the Next.js framework module does
   750→+- How framework detection and scanning flow works
   751→+- What each file in `desloppify/languages/typescript/frameworks/nextjs/` is responsible for
   752→+- Which shared files outside this folder affect behavior
   753→+- Current limits and safe extension points
   754→+
   755→+If you are new to this code, start with the `detect_nextjs_framework_smells` section and then read the "Scan flow" section.
   756→+
   757→+## High-level purpose
   758→+
   759→+The Next.js framework module adds framework-aware smells that generic code-quality detectors do not catch.
   760→+
   761→+Current scope includes:
   762→+
   763→+- App Router vs Pages Router migration and misuse signals
   764→+- Client/server boundary misuse (`"use client"`, `"use server"`, server-only imports/exports)
   765→+- Route handler and middleware context misuse
   766→+- Next.js API misuse in wrong router contexts
   767→+- Environment variable leakage in client modules
   768→+- `next lint` integration as a framework quality gate (`next_lint` detector)
   769→+
   770→+This module is intentionally heuristic-heavy (regex/file-structure based) so scans remain fast and robust without requiring full compiler semantics.
   771→+
   772→+## Module map
   773→+
   774→+Files in this folder:
   775→+
   776→+- `desloppify/languages/typescript/frameworks/nextjs/__init__.py`
   777→+- `desloppify/languages/typescript/frameworks/nextjs/info.py`
   778→+- `desloppify/languages/typescript/frameworks/nextjs/phase.py`
   779→+- `desloppify/languages/typescript/frameworks/nextjs/scanners.py`
   780→+
   781→+### What each file does
   782→+
   783→+`__init__.py`:
   784→+
   785→+- Exposes the framework info contract and shared phase entrypoint for imports
   786→+
   787→+`info.py`:
   788→+
   789→+- Defines `NextjsFrameworkInfo`
   790→+- Converts generic framework detection output into Next.js-specific router roots and flags
   791→+
   792→+`phase.py`:
   793→+
   794→+- Orchestrates all Next.js scanners
   795→+- Converts scanner entries into normalized issues (`nextjs` detector)
   796→+- Runs `next lint` integration and emits normalized lint issues (`next_lint` detector)
   797→+
   798→+`scanners.py`:
   799→+
   800→+- Implements all Next.js smell scanners
```

```
800→+- Implements all Next.js smell scanners
   801→+- Performs fast source-file discovery and content heuristics
   802→+- Returns normalized scanner entries for the phase orchestrator
   803→+
   804→+## Shared surfaces outside this folder
   805→+
   806→+These files are part of the same feature boundary and should be considered together:
   807→+
   808→+- `desloppify/languages/typescript/frameworks/detect.py`
   809→+- `desloppify/languages/typescript/frameworks/catalog.py`
   810→+- `desloppify/languages/typescript/frameworks/types.py`
   811→+- `desloppify/languages/typescript/phases_smells.py`
   812→+- `desloppify/languages/javascript/phases_nextjs.py`
   813→+- `desloppify/languages/_framework/node/next_lint.py`
   814→+- `desloppify/base/discovery/source.py`
   815→+
   816→+### Responsibility split
   817→+
   818→+- `frameworks/detect.py` decides whether Next.js is the primary framework and where package roots are.
   819→+- `nextjs/info.py` exposes Next.js routing context (`app_roots`, `pages_roots`) used by scanners.
   820→+- `nextjs/scanners.py` only finds smell candidates.
   821→+- `nextjs/phase.py` maps candidates to detector issues and handles scoring potentials.
   822→+- `next_lint.py` provides lint execution/parsing, independent of smell mapping.
   823→+
   824→+## Detectors
   825→+
   826→+This module emits findings under:
   827→+
   828→+- `nextjs`
   829→+- `next_lint`
   830→+
   831→+Registry/scoring wiring lives outside this folder in:
   832→+
   833→+- `desloppify/base/registry/catalog_entries.py`
   834→+- `desloppify/base/registry/catalog_models.py`
   835→+- `desloppify/engine/_scoring/policy/core.py`
   836→+
   837→+## Scan flow in plain language
   838→+
   839→+When TypeScript or JavaScript code smells run for a Next.js project, flow is:
   840→+
   841→+1. Framework detection picks primary framework from package markers and scripts.
   842→+2. Next.js info derives App/Pages router roots from detection evidence.
   843→+3. Shared Next.js phase runs all scanner functions.
   844→+4. Scanner entries are mapped into normalized `nextjs` issues.
   845→+5. `next lint` is executed and mapped into `next_lint` issues.
   846→+6. Potentials are returned for scoring and state merge.
   847→+
   848→+## `next lint` behavior
   849→+
   850→+`run_next_lint(...)` executes:
   851→+
   852→+- `npx --no-install next lint --format json`
   853→+
   854→+Behavior:
   855→+
   856→+- If lint runs and returns JSON, file-level lint findings are emitted.
   857→+- If lint cannot run or output cannot be parsed, a `next_lint::unavailable` issue is emitted.
   858→+
   859→+This keeps lint participation explicit in Next.js scans instead of silently skipping.
   860→+
   861→+## Smell families covered
   862→+
   863→+Current high-value families include:
   864→+
   865→+- `"use client"` placement and missing directive checks
   866→+- `"use server"` placement checks (module-level misuse only)
   867→+- Server-only imports in client modules (`next/headers`, `next/server`, `next/cache`, `server-only`, Node built-ins)
   868→+- Server-only Next exports from client modules (`metadata`, `generateMetadata`, `revalidate`, `dynamic`, etc.)
   869→+- Pages Router APIs used under App Router (`getServerSideProps`, `getStaticProps`, etc.)
   870→+- `next/navigation` usage in Pages Router files
   871→+- App Router metadata/config exports in Pages Router files
   872→+- Pages API route files exporting App Router route-handler HTTP functions
   873→+- App Router route handler and middleware misuse
   874→+- `next/head` usage in App Router
   875→+- `next/document` imports outside valid `_document.*` pages context
   876→+- Browser global usage in App Router modules missing `"use client"`
   877→+- Client layout smell and async client component smell
   878→+- Mixed `app/` and `pages/` router project smell
   879→+- Env leakage in client modules via non-`NEXT_PUBLIC_*` `process.env` usage
   880→+
   881→+## Extending this module safely
   882→+
   883→+When adding a new smell:
   884→+
   885→+1. Add scanner logic in `scanners.py`.
   886→+2. Return compact entries (`file`, `line`, and minimal structured detail).
   887→+3. Map entries to `make_issue(...)` in `phase.py` with clear `id`, `summary`, and `detail`.
   888→+4. Update/extend tests in:
   889→+   - `desloppify/languages/typescript/tests/test_ts_nextjs_framework.py`
   890→+   - `desloppify/languages/javascript/tests/test_js_nextjs_framework.py` (if JS parity applies)
   891→+5. Keep logic shared (do not duplicate TS vs JS framework smell rules).
   892→+
   893→+## Limits and tradeoffs
   894→+
   895→+- Scanners are heuristic, not compiler-accurate.
   896→+- Some patterns are intentionally conservative to avoid noisy false positives.
   897→+- Router/middleware checks rely on conventional Next.js file placement.
   898→+- `next lint` requires project dependencies to be present for full lint execution.
   899→+
   900→+These tradeoffs are deliberate: fast scans with high-signal framework smells, while preserving a clear extension path when stronger analysis is needed.

   973→+"""Next.js framework info derived from generic TypeScript framework detection."""
   974→+
   975→+from __future__ import annotations
   976→+
   977→+from dataclasses import dataclass
   978→+from pathlib import Path
   979→+from typing import Any
   980→+
   981→+from desloppify.languages.typescript.frameworks.types import PrimaryFrameworkDetection
   982→+
   983→+
   984→+@dataclass(frozen=True)
   985→+class NextjsFrameworkInfo:
   986→+    is_primary: bool
   987→+    package_root: Path
   988→+    package_json_relpath: str | None
   989→+    app_roots: tuple[str, ...]
   990→+    pages_roots: tuple[str, ...]
   991→+
   992→+    @property
   993→+    def uses_app_router(self) -> bool:
   994→+        return bool(self.app_roots)
   995→+
   996→+    @property
   997→+    def uses_pages_router(self) -> bool:
   998→+        return bool(self.pages_roots)
   999→+
  1000→+
  1001→+def _tuple_str(value: Any) -> tuple[str, ...]:
  1002→+    if not isinstance(value, list):
  1003→+        return ()
  1004→+    return tuple(str(v) for v in value if isinstance(v, str))
  1005→+
  1006→+
  1007→+def nextjs_info_from_detection(result: PrimaryFrameworkDetection) -> NextjsFrameworkInfo:
  1008→+    candidate = next((c for c in result.candidates if c.id == "nextjs"), None)
  1009→+    evidence = candidate.evidence if candidate is not None else {}
  1010→+    return NextjsFrameworkInfo(
  1011→+        is_primary=(result.primary_id == "nextjs"),
  1012→+        package_root=result.package_root,
  1013→+        package_json_relpath=result.package_json_relpath,
  1014→+        app_roots=_tuple_str(evidence.get("app_roots")),
  1015→+        pages_roots=_tuple_str(evidence.get("pages_roots")),
  1016→+    )
  1017→+
  1018→+
  1019→+__all__ = ["NextjsFrameworkInfo", "nextjs_info_from_detection"]
  1020→+

  1594→+    return results, {"nextjs": nextjs_scanned, "next_lint": next_lint_potential}

  1722→+def _has_use_server_directive_at_top(content: str) -> bool:
  1723→+    first = _first_meaningful_line(content.splitlines())
  1724→+    return bool(first and _USE_SERVER_LINE_RE.match(first))
  1725→+
  1726→+
  1727→+def _find_use_server_directive_line_anywhere(content: str) -> int | None:
  1728→+    for idx, line in enumerate(content.splitlines()[:200], start=1):
  1729→+        if _USE_SERVER_LINE_RE.match(line.strip()):
  1730→+            return idx
  1731→+    return None
  1732→+
  1733→+
  1734→+def _first_meaningful_line(lines: list[str]) -> str | None:
  1735→+    """Return the first non-empty, non-comment-only line."""
  1736→+    in_block_comment = False
  1737→+    for line in lines[:80]:
  1738→+        s = line.strip()
  1739→+        if not s:
  1740→+            continue
  1741→+        if in_block_comment:
  1742→+            end = s.find("*/")
  1743→+            if end == -1:
  1744→+                continue
  1745→+            s = s[end + 2 :].strip()
  1746→+            in_block_comment = False
  1747→+            if not s:
  1748→+                continue
  1749→+        if s.startswith("//"):
  1750→+            continue
  1751→+        if s.startswith("/*"):
  1752→+            end = s.find("*/", 2)
  1753→+            if end == -1:
  1754→+                in_block_comment = True
  1755→+                continue
  1756→+            s = s[end + 2 :].strip()
  1757→+            if not s:
  1758→+                continue
  1759→+        return s
  1760→+    return None
  1761→+
  1762→+
  1763→+def _has_use_client_directive(content: str) -> bool:
  1764→+    first = _first_meaningful_line(content.splitlines())
  1765→+    return bool(first and _USE_CLIENT_RE.match(first))
  1766→+
  1767→+
  1768→+def _find_use_client_directive_anywhere(content: str) -> int | None:
  1769→+    for idx, line in enumerate(content.splitlines()[:120], start=1):
  1770→+        if _USE_CLIENT_RE.match(line.strip()):
  1771→+            return idx
  1772→+    return None
  1773→+
  1774→+
  1775→+def _is_under_any_root(filepath: str, roots: tuple[str, ...]) -> bool:
  1776→+    return any(filepath == root or filepath.startswith(root.rstrip("/") + "/") for root in roots)
  1777→+
  1778→+
  1779→+def _iter_import_specifiers(search_text: str) -> list[dict]:
  1780→+    matches: list[dict] = []
  1781→+    for match in _MODULE_SPECIFIER_RE.finditer(search_text):
  1782→+        module = match.group("from") or match.group("require") or match.group("import") or ""
  1783→+        if not module:
  1784→+            continue
  1785→+        line_no = search_text[: match.start()].count("\n") + 1
  1786→+        matches.append({"module": module, "line": line_no})
  1787→+    return matches

  1790→+def _is_node_builtin(module: str) -> bool:
  1791→+    raw = module[5:] if module.startswith("node:") else module
  1792→+    base = raw.split("/", 1)[0]
  1793→+    return base in _NODE_BUILTIN_MODULES
  1794→+

  1796→+def _has_use_server_directive_anywhere(content: str) -> int | None:
  1797→+    for idx, line in enumerate(content.splitlines()[:500], start=1):
  1798→+        if _USE_SERVER_LINE_RE.match(line):
  1799→+            return idx
  1800→+        literal = _USE_SERVER_LITERAL_RE.search(line)
  1801→+        if literal:

  1806→+def _find_misplaced_module_use_server_directive(content: str) -> int | None:
  1807→+    """Find module-level 'use server' directives that are not first.
  1808→+
  1809→+    Intentionally ignores nested inline server actions where `'use server'` is
  1810→+    inside a function body (valid Next.js pattern).
  1811→+    """
  1812→+    search_text = _strip_ts_comments(content)
  1813→+    first_directive: str | None = None
  1814→+    in_prologue = True
  1815→+
  1816→+    for idx, line in enumerate(search_text.splitlines()[:300], start=1):
  1817→+        if not line.strip():
  1818→+            continue
  1819→+
  1820→+        stripped = line.strip()
  1821→+        is_directive = bool(_DIRECTIVE_LINE_RE.match(stripped))
  1822→+        is_use_server = bool(_USE_SERVER_LINE_RE.match(stripped))
  1823→+
  1824→+        if in_prologue:
  1825→+            if is_directive:
  1826→+                if first_directive is None:
  1827→+                    first_directive = stripped
  1828→+                if is_use_server and first_directive != stripped:
  1829→+                    return idx
  1830→+                continue
  1831→+            in_prologue = False
  1832→+
  1833→+        # Top-level misplaced directive after code starts.
  1834→+        if line == line.lstrip() and is_use_server:
  1835→+            return idx
  1836→+
  1837→+    return None

  1850→+def scan_nextjs_error_files_missing_use_client(
  1851→+    path: Path, info: NextjsFrameworkInfo

  1862→+            continue

  1864→+            continue
  1865→+
  1866→+        scanned += 1
  1867→+        try:
  1868→+            full = Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else get_project_root() / filepath
  1869→+            content = full.read_text()
  1870→+        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
  1871→+            logger.debug("Skipping unreadable Next.js candidate %s: %s", filepath, exc)
  1872→+            continue

  1874→+        if _has_use_client_directive(content):

  1878→+
  1879→+        entries.append({"file": filepath, "line": 1, "name": Path(filepath).name})
  1880→+
  1881→+    return entries, scanned

## PR #413 Review: "Fix planner deadlock when triage goes stale mid-cycle"

### 1. Correctness: Does the fix solve the deadlock?

**Yes, the core logic is sound.** The deadlock scenario is: triage is stale (new review issues arrived), but triage can't start because objective backlog is still open, and objective resolves are blocked because triage is stale. The PR breaks this cycle by:

- Detecting the "pending behind objective backlog" state (stale triage + no triage in queue + mid-cycle + objective backlog exists)
- Allowing objective `fixed` resolves to proceed in that state (guardrails.py:136-146)
- Keeping review resolves blocked (guardrails.py:148-157)

The four-part condition at `guardrails.py:55-59` correctly identifies the deadlock state:
- `not snapshot.has_triage_in_queue` -- triage stages aren't already queued
- `bool(resolved_state)` -- state is available
- `is_mid_cycle(resolved_plan)` -- we're in an active cycle
- `has_objective_backlog(resolved_state, None)` -- there's still objective work to do

This is the right set of conditions.

### 2. Code Quality

**Generally clean and follows existing patterns well.** A few observations:

- **`_REVIEW_DETECTORS` at guardrails.py:23**: Uses `frozenset({"review", "concerns"})` which is a subset of `NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS` (which also includes `"subjective_review"` and `"subjective_assessment"`). This is intentional — the guard only needs to check for direct review work, not all non-objective detectors — but there's a subtle coupling risk. If a new review-like detector is added to `NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS`, someone would need to remember to update `_REVIEW_DETECTORS` too. Consider adding a comment explaining why this is a subset, or importing and intersecting with `NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS`.

- **Duplicated condition logic**: The four-part `pending_behind_objective_backlog` check appears in both `guardrails.py:55-59` and `plan_triage.py:83-87`. This is a DRY concern — if the definition of "pending behind objective backlog" changes, both sites need updating. The `triage_phase_banner` function in `plan_triage.py` could potentially consume the `TriageGuardrailResult.pending_behind_objective_backlog` field instead of reimplementing the check, but I understand the banner function is a lower-level engine function that shouldn't depend on the app-layer guardrails. Still worth a comment cross-referencing the two sites.

- **`cmd.py:77` line length**: The line `plan=plan_access.plan if isinstance(plan_access.plan, dict) and not plan_access.degraded else None` is quite long. Not a blocker, but could be cleaner as a local variable.

- **lifecycle.py change (line 65)**: The copy change from "Cannot start triage while objective backlog is still open" to "Triage is pending behind the current objective backlog" is good — it aligns the language across all surfaces and removes the judgmental "Cannot" framing.

### 3. Test Coverage

**Good coverage of the core cases, but a few gaps:**

Tests cover:
- `triage_guardrail_status` marks pending state correctly
- `triage_guardrail_messages` uses the right copy
- Objective resolve is allowed while pending
- Review resolve is blocked while pending

**Missing test cases:**
- **No patterns provided**: `require_triage_current_or_exit` with `pending_behind_objective_backlog=True` but `patterns=None` or `patterns=[]`. This falls through to the `result.pending_behind_objective_backlog` block at line 149 and raises `CommandError`. Should have a test confirming this path.
- **Mixed patterns**: What happens when patterns match both objective and review issues? `_targets_include_review_work` returns `True` if *any* target is review work, so the whole batch gets blocked. This is correct (conservative), but a test documenting the behavior would be valuable.
- **Bypass with attestation**: No test that `bypass=True` with a 30+ char attestation still works in the pending state (it should, since the bypass check is before the pending check).
- **Banner test in `test_triage_phase_banner.py`**: The new test at line 31 is good but doesn't verify the exact banner text contains the "activate after objective work" messaging — it only checks `startswith("TRIAGE PENDING")`.
- **`_matched_open_targets` with no matching issues**: If patterns don't match any open issues, `matched_targets` is empty, so the `if matched_targets` check at line 138 is `False`, and it falls through to the `CommandError` at line 149. This is correct but untested.

### 4. Concerns

**Minor issues:**

1. **`_matched_open_targets` calls `state_mod.match_issues`** (guardrails.py:183) which is `desloppify.state` — this is an import of the top-level `state` module at line 8 (`from desloppify import state as state_mod`). This is an app-layer file so the import direction is fine, but it's the only place in guardrails.py that uses `state_mod`. The import was added cleanly.

2. **`result._plan or {}`** at guardrails.py:140: The `_plan` field is `dict | None` with default `None`. If `pending_behind_objective_backlog` is `True`, then `triage_guardrail_status` succeeded in loading a plan, so `result._plan` will always be a `dict` here — the `or {}` fallback is defensive but technically unreachable in this branch. Not a bug, just noise.

3. **The `__all__` list in guardrails.py** (line 194) does not include the two new private helpers (`_matched_open_targets`, `_targets_include_review_work`). This is correct since they're private, but `_REVIEW_DETECTORS` is also not exported, which is fine.

4. **No edge case for empty `new_ids`**: If `is_triage_stale` is `True` but `new_since_triage_ids` is empty (which can happen if staleness is flagged by `triage_recommended` rather than actual new issues), `pending_behind_objective_backlog` would still be `True` but `result.new_ids` would be empty. The messaging path in `triage_guardrail_messages` only fires `if result.new_ids`, so the pending copy would be skipped but the banner would still show. The `require_triage_current_or_exit` path would still block review resolves though. This seems like a rare edge case but worth considering.

### Summary

This is a well-structured fix for a real deadlock. The logic is correct, the code follows existing patterns, and the test coverage hits the main cases. The PR is approval-worthy with minor suggestions:

- Add a comment on `_REVIEW_DETECTORS` explaining why it's a subset of `NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS`
- Consider a comment cross-referencing the duplicated pending-detection logic between `guardrails.py:55-59` and `plan_triage.py:83-87`
- Add a test for the "no patterns" and "mixed patterns" paths through `require_triage_current_or_exit`
- Optional: extract the long conditional in `cmd.py:77` into a local variable for readability

  2825→+"""TypeScript framework detection contracts."""
  2826→+

  2835→+

  2930→+def _write(tmp_path: Path, name: str, content: str) -> Path:
  2931→+    p = tmp_path / name
  2932→+    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  2933→+    p.write_text(content)
  2934→+    return p

  2937→+def test_detect_nextjs_framework_primary_when_next_and_app_present(tmp_path: Path):
  2938→+    _write(tmp_path, "package.json", '{"dependencies": {"next": "14.0.0"}}\n')
  2939→+    _write(tmp_path, "app/page.tsx", "export default function Page() { return <div/> }\n")
  2940→+
  2941→+    framework = detect_primary_ts_framework(tmp_path)
  2942→+    info = nextjs_info_from_detection(framework)
  2943→+    assert info.is_primary is True
  2944→+    assert info.uses_app_router is True
  2945→+    assert "app" in info.app_roots
  2946→+
  2947→+
  2948→+def test_detect_nextjs_framework_not_primary_when_only_app_tree_exists(tmp_path: Path):
  2949→+    _write(tmp_path, "package.json", '{"dependencies": {"react": "18.3.0"}}\n')
  2950→+    _write(tmp_path, "app/page.tsx", "export default function Page() { return <div/> }\n")
  2951→+
  2952→+    framework = detect_primary_ts_framework(tmp_path)
  2953→+    info = nextjs_info_from_detection(framework)
  2954→+    assert info.is_primary is False
  2955→+
  2956→+
  2957→+def test_detect_nextjs_framework_primary_for_external_scan_path(tmp_path: Path):
  2958→+    external = tmp_path.parent / f"{tmp_path.name}-external-next"
  2959→+    external.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  2960→+    (external / "package.json").write_text('{"dependencies": {"next": "14.0.0"}}\n')
  2961→+    (external / "app").mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  2962→+    (external / "app" / "page.tsx").write_text("export default function Page(){return <div/>}\n")
  2963→+
  2964→+    framework = detect_primary_ts_framework(external)
  2965→+    info = nextjs_info_from_detection(framework)
  2966→+    assert info.is_primary is True
  2967→+    assert info.package_root == external.resolve()
  2968→+    assert info.package_json_relpath is not None
  2969→+    assert info.package_json_relpath.endswith("package.json")
  2970→+
  2971→+
  2972→+def test_use_server_not_first_ignores_nested_inline_actions(tmp_path: Path):
  2973→+    _write(tmp_path, "package.json", '{"dependencies": {"next": "14.0.0"}}\n')
  2974→+    _write(
  2975→+        tmp_path,
  2976→+        "app/inline-action.tsx",
  2977→+        (
  2978→+            "export default async function Page() {\n"
  2979→+            "  async function doAction() {\n"
  2980→+            "    'use server'\n"
  2981→+            "    return 1\n"
  2982→+            "  }\n"
  2983→+            "  return <div>{String(!!doAction)}</div>\n"
  2984→+            "}\n"
  2985→+        ),
  2986→+    )
  2987→+    _write(
  2988→+        tmp_path,
  2989→+        "app/misplaced.ts",
  2990→+        "export const x = 1\n'use server'\nexport async function action(){ return 1 }\n",
  2991→+    )
  2992→+
  2993→+    framework = detect_primary_ts_framework(tmp_path)
  2994→+    info = nextjs_info_from_detection(framework)
  2995→+    entries, _ = scan_nextjs_use_server_not_first(tmp_path, info)
  2996→+    files = {entry["file"] for entry in entries}
  2997→+    assert "app/misplaced.ts" in files
  2998→+    assert "app/inline-action.tsx" not in files

## PR #415 Review: "fix: improve cxx detector scoping"

**Author:** Dragoy | **Stats:** +333 / -33 across 10 files

### Summary

Three related improvements to the C/C++ language plugin:
1. **Security finding scoping** — filter out findings from external headers (e.g. vendor) that tools like clang-tidy report but aren't in the scanned file set.
2. **CMake-based test coverage mapping** — parse `CMakeLists.txt` to discover which source files a test target compiles, treating that as direct test coverage.
3. **Disable unused-imports phase for C++** — the generic tree-sitter "Unused imports" detector is unsound for C/C++ `#include` semantics.

Plus a fix to `_extract_import_name` so it handles C++ header extensions (`.h`, `.hh`, `.hpp`) and changes separator priority so `/` and `\` are stripped before `::` and `.`.

---

### 1. Correctness

**Security scoping** (`security.py:662-667`): The approach of resolving both the entry's file path and the scoped file paths, then filtering by membership, is correct. One concern:

- **Issue (minor):** The `entry.get("file", "")` is cast to `str()` then to `Path(...).resolve()`. If `entry["file"]` is already an absolute resolved path (which it should be from the tool parsers), the double-resolve is harmless but redundant. If it's empty string, `Path("").resolve()` returns the CWD, which won't be in `scoped_file_set` — so that's safely filtered. Fine.

**CMake coverage mapping** (`test_coverage.py`): The `discover_test_mapping_files` function walks up from each test file to the nearest `tests/`/`test/` ancestor directory, collecting `CMakeLists.txt` and `Makefile` files. These are then fed into `parse_test_import_specs`, which now also parses CMake `add_executable`/`add_library`/`target_sources` commands.

- The `_CMAKE_SOURCE_SPEC_RE` regex correctly handles both quoted and unquoted source specs. It strips CMake comments first. This is a reasonable heuristic for common CMake patterns.
- **Note:** The `_iter_test_tree_ancestors` function returns an empty list if no `tests/`/`test/` directory is found in the path. This is intentional — it means CMake discovery only activates within recognized test trees.

**Unused imports exclusion** (`__init__.py:52-55`): Filtering by `phase.label != "Unused imports"` is a string comparison against the label set in the tree-sitter phase builder. This is fragile but consistent with how other parts of the codebase reference phases by label.

**`_extract_import_name` refactor** (`unused_imports.py:147-174`): The old code processed all separators (`::`, `.`, `/`, `\`) in a single priority loop — the first separator found would win. The problem: `WidgetCatalog.hpp` would split on `.` and return `hpp`. The new code:
1. First strips `/` and `\` (path separators)
2. Then strips file extensions (now including `.h`, `.hh`, `.hpp`)
3. Then splits on `::` and `.` (namespace separators)

This ordering is correct for C++ includes like `vendor/json.hpp` -> strip path -> `json.hpp` -> strip ext -> `json`.

---

### 2. Code Quality

- **Blank line additions:** There are ~15 extra blank lines added between functions throughout `test_coverage.py` and `mapping_imports.py`. These add no value and create noise in the diff. The codebase standard is one blank line between module-level functions (PEP 8 says two, but this codebase uses one). The PR adds a third blank line in several places. Minor but worth a cleanup pass.

- **Duplicate test:** `test_detect_cxx_security_ignores_findings_outside_scanned_files` (line ~93 of test_security.py) and `test_detect_cxx_security_ignores_findings_outside_scoped_files` (line ~613) are nearly identical — same setup, same assertions, same monkeypatching. The only difference is one says "scanned" and the other "scoped" in the name. One of these should be removed.

- **`_unique_preserving_order`** in `test_coverage.py`: Clean utility, but it's only called from `parse_test_import_specs` and `_parse_cmake_source_specs`. The implementation is fine.

- **Missing newline at EOF** in `test_init.py` (line 46) and `test_security.py` (last line). These will likely fail a linting check.

- The `_discover_additional_test_mapping_files` function is exported in `mapping_imports.__all__` with a leading underscore, which is unusual. It's a private-looking name in a public export list. Since it's called from `detector.py` (a different module), the export is correct, but the naming is inconsistent.

---

### 3. Test Coverage

Tests are generally good:

- `test_parse_test_import_specs_extracts_cmake_sources` — verifies CMake parsing directly
- `test_discover_test_mapping_files_finds_cmakelists_within_test_tree` — verifies the file discovery walk
- `test_detect_test_coverage_uses_cmake_test_sources_for_direct_mapping` — integration test through the full detector, asserting the production file is marked as covered
- `test_cxx_excludes_unused_imports_phase` — verifies phase filtering
- `test_detect_cxx_security_ignores_findings_outside_scanned_files` — verifies external header filtering
- `_extract_import_name` assertions for `.hpp` files added to the existing helper test

**Missing test coverage:**
- No test for `_iter_test_tree_ancestors` when there's no `tests/`/`test/` directory in the path (the empty-list case). This is important because it silently disables CMake discovery for non-standard test directory names.
- No test for `_parse_cmake_source_specs` with `target_sources()` command — only `add_executable` and `add_library` are tested indirectly.
- No test for CMake files with comments that should be stripped (the `_CMAKE_COMMENT_RE` path).

---

### 4. Concerns

**a. Performance of security filtering (`security.py:662-665`):** The `scoped_file_set` is built by resolving every file path. Then for each entry, `Path(str(entry.get("file", ""))).resolve()` is called. This is O(n*m) in the worst case with path resolution syscalls. For typical C++ projects with hundreds of files and dozens of findings, this is fine. For very large projects, the double `str(Path(...).resolve())` pattern could be slow. Not a blocker.

**b. `parse_test_import_specs` now parses CMake in addition to `#include`:** If a CMake file is fed to this function (via the mapping files discovery), the `#include` regex will match nothing (no `#include` in CMakeLists.txt), and the CMake regex will extract source specs. But if a `.cpp` test file happens to contain text matching `_CMAKE_SOURCE_SPEC_RE` (e.g., a string literal with a CMake-like pattern), it could produce spurious specs. This seems unlikely in practice.

**c. The `detect_test_coverage` change in `detector.py` (line 56-68):** The logic change is subtle. Previously, `naming_based_mapping` was only called `if test_files`. Now it's still gated by `if test_files` (line 68), but `import_based_mapping` is called with `mapping_test_files` (which includes CMake files) and gated by `if mapping_test_files`. Since `mapping_test_files` starts as `set(test_files)` and only grows, if `test_files` is non-empty then `mapping_test_files` is also non-empty. If `test_files` is empty, `mapping_test_files` stays empty (the discovery function needs test files to walk from). So the logic is equivalent for the empty case. Correct.

---

### Verdict

The PR addresses real problems (external header noise in security findings, missing CMake-based coverage, unsound unused-import detection for C++). The implementation is sound. Main action items:

1. **Remove one of the duplicate security tests** (lines ~93 vs ~613 of `test_security.py`)
2. **Fix missing newlines at EOF** in `test_init.py` and `test_security.py`
3. **Clean up the spurious extra blank lines** — optional but noisy
4. Consider adding a test for `_parse_cmake_source_specs` with comments and `target_sources`

## PR #414 Review: Next.js Framework Detection + `next lint` Integration

### Overall Assessment

This is a well-structured, large PR (~3300 lines of diff, 23 files changed) that adds a complete Next.js framework detection pipeline. The architecture is sound and the code is clean. However, the PR size warrants consideration for splitting, and there are several specific issues worth addressing.

---

### 1. Architecture

**Strengths:**
- The layered separation is excellent: `types.py` (contracts) -> `catalog.py` (data-driven signatures) -> `detect.py` (generic scoring) -> `nextjs/info.py` (framework-specific adapter) -> `nextjs/scanners.py` (heuristics) -> `nextjs/phase.py` (issue mapping). This is easy to extend to new frameworks.
- The shared `_framework/node/` location for `next_lint.py` is the right place for cross-language Node tooling.
- Caching via `lang.review_cache` avoids redundant detection across TS/JS.

**Issues:**

**(a) JS plugin importing directly from TS plugin internals** -- `desloppify/languages/javascript/phases_nextjs.py` lines 6-10 imports `detect_primary_ts_framework`, `nextjs_info_from_detection`, and `detect_nextjs_framework_smells` directly from the TypeScript plugin package. This creates a hard cross-language-plugin coupling. The function is named `detect_primary_ts_framework` but is being used for JavaScript projects. The detection logic (package.json reading, scoring) is actually language-agnostic. Consider moving the shared framework detection layer to `languages/_framework/` or at minimum renaming the function to drop the `_ts_` qualifier. Right now a change in the TS plugin's framework internals can break the JS plugin silently.

**(b) `phase.py` imports from `engine._state.filtering`** -- `desloppify/languages/typescript/frameworks/nextjs/phase.py` line 7 imports `make_issue` from `desloppify.engine._state.filtering`. Per the project's import direction rules, `languages/` importing from `engine/` is allowed (languages -> engine/detectors), so this is technically fine, but importing from a private `_state` submodule is fragile. If `make_issue` is a stable interface, it should either be re-exported from a public surface or this pattern should be documented as accepted.

**(c) Vite signature included but not used** -- `catalog.py` lines 39-49 define a Vite `FrameworkSignature` but the PR has no Vite-specific scanners, no Vite detector in the registry, and no tests for Vite detection. This is dead code / forward-looking scaffolding. It could confuse future contributors who see Vite detected but never acted upon.

---

### 2. Correctness

**Strengths:**
- The `_find_misplaced_module_use_server_directive` logic (scanners.py lines ~180-210) correctly distinguishes between module-level `'use server'` (which must be first) and nested inline server actions inside function bodies (which are valid). This directly addresses the false-positive fix mentioned in the PR description.
- The `_find_nearest_package_json` upward traversal with project-root capping (detect.py lines ~7-33) properly handles both in-workspace and external scan paths.
- The scoring threshold mechanism (default 5, requiring dep + marker or dep + config) prevents spurious detection for projects that merely have an `app/` directory.

**Issues:**

**(d) `_has_use_server_directive_anywhere` has a false-positive risk** -- `scanners.py` function around line ~170: it matches `_USE_SERVER_LITERAL_RE` (just the string `'use server'` anywhere on a line) in addition to `_USE_SERVER_LINE_RE`. This means a comment like `// don't use 'use server' here` or a string assignment `const x = "use server"` would match. This function is used by `scan_nextjs_use_server_in_client` to flag client modules with `'use server'` -- so a client module containing the string "use server" in a comment or variable value would be falsely flagged.

**(e) `scan_nextjs_use_server_in_client` flags valid inline server actions** -- `scanners.py`, the `scan_nextjs_use_server_in_client` function: a `'use client'` module is allowed to contain functions with `'use server'` inside the function body (inline server actions). This is a valid Next.js 14+ pattern. The scanner flags any `'use server'` presence in a `'use client'` module, which would produce false positives for this valid pattern. The PR already correctly handles the reverse case (module-level misplacement) in `_find_misplaced_module_use_server_directive` but doesn't apply the same indentation/nesting awareness here.

**(f) `_extract_json_array` is fragile** -- `next_lint.py` lines ~19-27: using `text.find("[")` / `text.rfind("]")` to extract JSON could match bracket characters in error messages, log output, or other non-JSON content that `next lint` might write to stdout/stderr. A more robust approach would be to try `json.loads` on progressively smaller substrings, or at least verify the extracted substring starts with `[{`.

**(g) `scan_nextjs_error_files_missing_use_client` only targets `.ts`/`.tsx`** -- The `targets` set at line ~228 only includes `error.tsx`, `error.ts`, `global-error.tsx`, `global-error.ts` but not the `.js`/`.jsx` equivalents. Since this scanner is shared with the JS plugin (which deals with `.js`/`.jsx` files), this means JS Next.js projects would not get error boundary file checks. Same issue applies to `_is_middleware` which only checks `.ts`/`.tsx` suffixes.

**(h) No `--skip-slow` integration for `next lint`** -- The `run_next_lint` call has a 180-second timeout and spawns a subprocess. The existing project has a `--skip-slow` flag for expensive operations. `next lint` (which requires building the project) should respect this flag, but there's no check for it in the phase orchestration. This could make scans unexpectedly slow.

---

### 3. Code Quality

**Strengths:**
- Consistent scanner function signatures: all return `tuple[list[dict], int]` (entries + scanned count).
- Good use of `_strip_ts_comments` and `_code_text` from existing helpers to reduce false positives on commented-out code.
- Defensive coding throughout (try/except on file reads, type checking on JSON payloads).

**Issues:**

**(i) Massive boilerplate in `phase.py`** -- The `detect_nextjs_framework_smells` function in `phase.py` is 572 lines and follows an identical pattern for each scanner: call scanner, iterate entries, `make_issue()`, log. This is ~25 copy-paste blocks with the same structure. A data-driven approach (list of scanner configs with id/tier/confidence/summary-template) could cut this to ~50 lines and eliminate the risk of copy-paste bugs. This is the single biggest code quality concern in the PR.

**(j) Duplicated file-reading boilerplate in scanners** -- Nearly every scanner function in `scanners.py` has the same 6-line block:
```python
full = Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else get_project_root() / filepath
content = full.read_text()
```
with identical exception handling. This should be extracted to a shared helper.

**(k) `nextjs/__init__.py` re-exports every scanner** -- The `__init__.py` (lines ~1-60) imports and re-exports all 22 scanner functions. Only `nextjs_info_from_detection` and `NextjsFrameworkInfo` are needed externally (by `phase.py` and the JS plugin). The scanner re-exports are unused outside the package and add maintenance burden (keeping `__all__` in sync).

**(l) Script-matching regex in `_score_signature` is framework-ID-specific** -- `detect.py` lines ~100-108: the script-matching logic uses `if sig.id == "nextjs"` / `elif sig.id == "vite"` hardcoded checks inside what's supposed to be a generic scoring function. This should be a field on `FrameworkSignature` (e.g., `script_pattern: str | None`).

---

### 4. Test Coverage

**Strengths:**
- The TS test (`test_ts_nextjs_framework.py`) is thorough: 385 lines covering framework detection, external path handling, inline server action false-positive guarding, and a comprehensive integration test that verifies all 22+ issue IDs are emitted.
- The JS test verifies phase registration and end-to-end smell emission through the JS plugin path.
- Existing tests updated appropriately (unused detector error message, work queue registry).

**Gaps:**

**(m) No unit tests for `run_next_lint`** -- The `next_lint.py` module has no dedicated tests. Tests only monkeypatch it away (`lambda _root: ([], 0, "tests disabled")`). The JSON extraction, relativization, and error handling paths should be tested independently.

**(n) No tests for framework detection edge cases** -- Missing: Vite vs Next.js scoring tie-breaking, `package.json` with malformed JSON, monorepo with nested `package.json` files, `devDependencies`-only Next.js (common in some setups).

**(o) No negative tests for individual scanners** -- The test creates files that should trigger each scanner, but doesn't verify that correct files are NOT flagged (except the inline server action test). For example, no test that a properly-placed `'use client'` file with `useState` is clean.

---

### 5. Size / Scope

This PR adds ~2800 new lines across 17 new files plus modifications to 6 existing files. It bundles:

1. Generic framework detection infrastructure (types, catalog, detect, scoring)
2. Next.js-specific scanner suite (22 scanners)
3. `next lint` subprocess integration
4. JS plugin parity wiring
5. TS `tsc` fallback chain improvement (unrelated to Next.js)
6. Detector registry + scoring policy wiring

**Recommendation:** The PR should ideally be split into at least 2-3 pieces:
- **PR A**: Generic framework detection layer (`frameworks/types.py`, `catalog.py`, `detect.py`) + registry/scoring wiring -- small, reviewable, mergeable independently.
- **PR B**: Next.js scanners + phase + `next lint` + tests -- the meat of the feature.
- **PR C**: The `tsc` fallback chain change in `unused.py` is unrelated and should be its own PR.

The current single PR makes it harder to review the generic detection infrastructure independently from the Next.js-specific implementation.

---

### Summary of Actionable Items

| Priority | Issue | Location |
|----------|-------|----------|
| High | (e) `scan_nextjs_use_server_in_client` false-positives on valid inline server actions | `scanners.py` |
| High | (d) `_has_use_server_directive_anywhere` matches string literals/comments | `scanners.py` ~line 170 |
| High | (g) Error/middleware scanners miss `.js`/`.jsx` extensions | `scanners.py` ~line 228, ~line 331 |
| Medium | (i) 572-line copy-paste `phase.py` should be data-driven | `nextjs/phase.py` |
| Medium | (a) JS plugin hard-coupled to TS plugin internals | `javascript/phases_nextjs.py` |
| Medium | (h) No `--skip-slow` check before running `next lint` | `nextjs/phase.py` ~line 48 |
| Medium | (m) No unit tests for `run_next_lint` | `_framework/node/next_lint.py` |
| Low | (j) Duplicated file-reading boilerplate | `scanners.py` (throughout) |
| Low | (k) Unnecessary re-exports in `__init__.py` | `nextjs/__init__.py` |
| Low | (l) Hardcoded framework IDs in generic scoring | `detect.py` ~line 100 |
| Low | (c) Dead Vite signature with no consumers | `catalog.py` |
| Suggestion | Split into 2-3 smaller PRs | -- |

1→"""Core language-framework dataclasses and contracts."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Callable
     6→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Protocol
     9→
    10→from desloppify.engine.detectors.base import FunctionInfo
    11→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.lang_config_runtime import (
    12→    clone_default,
    13→    coerce_value,
    14→    normalize_spec_values,
    15→    runtime_value,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types_shared import (
    18→    BoundaryRule,
    19→    CoverageStatus,
    20→    DetectorEntry,
    21→    DetectorCoverageRecord,
    22→    DetectorCoverageStatus,
    23→    FixerConfig,
    24→    FixResult,
    25→    LangSecurityResult,
    26→    LangValueSpec,
    27→    ScanCoverageRecord,
    28→)
    29→
    30→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    31→    from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import FileZoneMap, ZoneRule
    32→
    33→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    34→# Type aliases for complex Callable signatures used in LangConfig fields
    35→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    36→DepGraphBuilder = Callable[[Path], dict[str, dict[str, Any]]]
    37→FunctionExtractor = Callable[[Path], list[FunctionInfo]]
    38→FileFinder = Callable[[Path], list[str]]
    39→
    40→
    41→@dataclass
    42→class DetectorPhase:
    43→    """A single phase in the scan pipeline.
    44→
    45→    Each phase runs one or more detectors and returns normalized issues.
    46→    The `run` function handles both detection AND normalization (converting
    47→    raw detector output to issues with tiers/confidence).
    48→    """
    49→
    50→    label: str
    51→    run: Callable[[Path, LangRuntimeContract], tuple[list[DetectorEntry], dict[str, int]]]
    52→    slow: bool = False
    53→
    54→
    55→class LangRuntimeContract(Protocol):
    56→    """Explicit runtime interface consumed by detector phases.
    57→
    58→    This Protocol lives here (rather than in runtime.py) to avoid an import
    59→    cycle: runtime.py imports concrete types from this module, and
    60→    DetectorPhase.run references LangRuntimeContract in its signature.
    61→    """
    62→
    63→    name: str
    64→    extensions: list[str]
    65→    entry_patterns: list[str]
    66→    barrel_names: set[str]
    67→    external_test_dirs: list[str]
    68→    test_file_extensions: list[str]
    69→    review_low_value_pattern: object | None
    70→    file_finder: FileFinder | None
    71→    extract_functions: FunctionExtractor | None
    72→    get_area: Callable[[str], str] | None
    73→    build_dep_graph: DepGraphBuilder
    74→    detect_lang_security_detailed: Callable[[list[str], FileZoneMap | None], LangSecurityResult]
    75→    detect_private_imports: Callable[
    76→        [dict, FileZoneMap | None], tuple[list[DetectorEntry], int]
    77→    ]
    78→    large_threshold: int
    79→    complexity_threshold: int
    80→    props_threshold: int
    81→
    82→    zone_map: FileZoneMap | None
    83→    dep_graph: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] | None
    84→    complexity_map: dict[str, float]
    85→    review_cache: dict[str, Any]
    86→    review_max_age_days: int
    87→    subjective_assessments: dict[str, Any]
    88→    detector_coverage: dict[str, DetectorCoverageRecord]
    89→    coverage_warnings: list[DetectorCoverageRecord]
    90→
    91→    def runtime_setting(self, key: str, default: Any = None) -> Any: ...
    92→
    93→    def runtime_option(self, key: str, default: Any = None) -> Any: ...
    94→
    95→    def scan_coverage_prerequisites(self) -> list[DetectorCoverageStatus]: ...
    96→
    97→
    98→@dataclass
    99→class LangConfig:
   100→    """Language configuration — everything the pipeline needs to scan a codebase."""
   101→
   102→    name: str
   103→    extensions: list[str]
   104→    exclusions: list[str]
   105→    default_src: str  # relative to PROJECT_ROOT
   106→
   107→    # Dep graph builder (language-specific import parsing)
   108→    build_dep_graph: DepGraphBuilder
   109→
   110→    # Entry points (not orphaned even with 0 importers)
   111→    entry_patterns: list[str]
   112→    barrel_names: set[str]
   113→
   114→    # Detector phases (ordered)
   115→    phases: list[DetectorPhase] = field(default_factory=list)
   116→
   117→    # Fixer registry
   118→    fixers: dict[str, FixerConfig] = field(default_factory=dict)
   119→
   120→    # Area classification (project-specific grouping)
   121→    get_area: Callable[[str], str] | None = None
   122→
   123→    # Commands for `detect` subcommand (language-specific overrides)
   124→    # Keys serve as the valid detector name list.
   125→    detect_commands: dict[str, Callable[..., Any]] = field(default_factory=dict)
   126→
   127→    # Function extractor (for duplicate detection). Returns a list of FunctionInfo items.
   128→    extract_functions: FunctionExtractor | None = None
   129→
   130→    # Coupling boundaries (optional, project-specific)
   131→    boundaries: list[BoundaryRule] = field(default_factory=list)
   132→
   133→    # Unused detection tool command (for post-fix checklist)
   134→    typecheck_cmd: str = ""
   135→
   136→    # File finder: (path) -> list[str]
   137→    file_finder: FileFinder | None = None
   138→
   139→    # Structural analysis thresholds
   140→    large_threshold: int = 500
   141→    complexity_threshold: int = 15
   142→    props_threshold: int = 14
   143→    default_scan_profile: str = "full"
   144→
   145→    # Language-specific persisted settings and per-run runtime options.
   146→    setting_specs: dict[str, LangValueSpec] = field(default_factory=dict)
   147→    runtime_option_specs: dict[str, LangValueSpec] = field(default_factory=dict)
   148→
   149→    # Project-level files that indicate this language is present
   150→    detect_markers: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
   151→
   152→    # External test discovery (outside scanned path)
   153→    external_test_dirs: list[str] = field(default_factory=lambda: ["tests", "test"])
   154→    test_file_extensions: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
   155→
   156→    # Review-context language hooks
   157→    review_module_patterns_fn: Callable[[str], list[str]] | None = None
   158→    review_api_surface_fn: Callable[[dict[str, str]], dict] | None = None
   159→    review_guidance: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
   160→    review_low_value_pattern: object | None = None
   161→    holistic_review_dimensions: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
   162→    migration_pattern_pairs: list[tuple[str, object, object]] = field(
   163→        default_factory=list
   164→    )
   165→    migration_mixed_extensions: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
   166→
   167→    # Zone classification rules
   168→    zone_rules: list[ZoneRule] = field(default_factory=list)
   169→
   170→    # Integration depth: "full" | "standard" | "shallow" | "minimal"
   171→    integration_depth: str = "full"
   172→
   173→    _default_runtime_settings: dict[str, object] = field(
   174→        default_factory=dict, init=False, repr=False
   175→    )
   176→    _default_runtime_options: dict[str, object] = field(
   177→        default_factory=dict, init=False, repr=False
   178→    )
   179→
   180→    @staticmethod
   181→    def _clone_default(default: object) -> object:
   182→        return clone_default(default)
   183→
   184→    @classmethod
   185→    def _coerce_value(cls, raw: object, expected: type, default: object) -> object:
   186→        return coerce_value(raw, expected, default)
   187→
   188→    def normalize_settings(self, values: dict[str, object] | None) -> dict[str, object]:
   189→        return normalize_spec_values(values, self.setting_specs)
   190→
   191→    def normalize_runtime_options(
   192→        self,
   193→        values: dict[str, object] | None,
   194→        *,
   195→        strict: bool = False,
   196→    ) -> dict[str, object]:
   197→        return normalize_spec_values(
   198→            values,
   199→            self.runtime_option_specs,
   200→            strict=strict,
   201→            owner_name=self.name,
   202→        )
   203→
   204→    def set_runtime_context(
   205→        self,
   206→        *,
   207→        settings: dict[str, object] | None = None,
   208→        options: dict[str, object] | None = None,
   209→    ) -> None:
   210→        """Set default runtime settings/options for future LangRun creation."""
   211→        if settings is not None:
   212→            self._default_runtime_settings = self.normalize_settings(settings)
   213→        if options is not None:
   214→            self._default_runtime_options = self.normalize_runtime_options(options)
   215→
   216→    def runtime_setting(self, key: str, default: Any = None) -> Any:
   217→        """Read setting from config-level runtime defaults."""
   218→        return runtime_value(
   219→            self._default_runtime_settings,
   220→            self.setting_specs,
   221→            key,
   222→            default,
   223→        )
   224→
   225→    def runtime_option(self, key: str, default: Any = None) -> Any:
   226→        """Read option from config-level runtime defaults."""
   227→        return runtime_value(
   228→            self._default_runtime_options,
   229→            self.runtime_option_specs,
   230→            key,
   231→            default,
   232→        )
   233→
   234→    def detect_lang_security_detailed(
   235→        self,
   236→        files: list[str],
   237→        zone_map: FileZoneMap | None,
   238→    ) -> LangSecurityResult:
   239→        """Language-specific security checks with optional coverage metadata."""
   240→        return LangSecurityResult(entries=[], files_scanned=0)
   241→
   242→    def detect_private_imports(
   243→        self, graph: dict, zone_map: FileZoneMap | None
   244→    ) -> tuple[list[DetectorEntry], int]:
   245→        """Language-specific private-import detection. Override in subclasses."""
   246→        return [], 0
   247→
   248→    def scan_coverage_prerequisites(self) -> list[DetectorCoverageStatus]:
   249→        """Optional preflight checks that can reduce scan confidence."""
   250→        return []
   251→
   252→
   253→__all__ = [
   254→    "BoundaryRule",

    63→def _ts_treesitter_phases() -> list[DetectorPhase]:
    64→    """Cherry-pick tree-sitter phases that complement TS's own detectors."""
    65→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import get_spec, is_available
    66→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter.phases import make_cohesion_phase
    67→
    68→    if not is_available():
    69→        return []
    70→
    71→    spec = get_spec("typescript")
    72→    if spec is None:
    73→        return []
    74→
    75→    return [make_cohesion_phase(spec)]
    76→
    77→
    78→def _ts_extract_functions(path):
    79→    """Extract all TS functions for duplicate detection."""
    80→    from desloppify.base.discovery.source import find_ts_and_tsx_files
    81→
    82→    functions = []
    83→    for filepath in find_ts_and_tsx_files(path):
    84→        if "node_modules" in filepath or ".d.ts" in filepath:
    85→            continue
    86→        functions.extend(extract_ts_functions(filepath))
    87→    return functions

    95→            files_scanned=result.population_size,
    96→        )

     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations

     5→from functools import partial

     7→from .shared_phases import (

    19→def _make_detector_phase_factory(
    20→    label: str,
    21→    run_fn,
    22→    *,
    23→    slow: bool = False,
    24→    exclusive_detector: str | None = None,
    25→):
    26→    """Return a phase factory with explicit label/run/slow semantics.
    27→
    28→    *exclusive_detector*: if set, the detector module path that this phase owns.
    29→    Language-specific phase files must not import that module directly.
    30→    """
    31→    def factory() -> DetectorPhase:
    32→        return DetectorPhase(label, run_fn, slow=slow)
    33→    factory.exclusive_detector = exclusive_detector
    34→    return factory

    83→def shared_subjective_duplicates_tail(
    84→    *,
    85→    pre_duplicates: list[DetectorPhase] | None = None,
    86→) -> list[DetectorPhase]:
    87→    """Shared review tail: subjective review, optional custom phases, then duplicates."""

    91→    phases.append(detector_phase_boilerplate_duplication())

1→"""Generic language plugin system — run external tools, parse output, emit issues.
     2→
     3→Provides `generic_lang()` to register a language plugin from a list of tool specs.
     4→Each tool runs a shell command at scan time, parses the output into issues, and
     5→gracefully degrades when the tool is not installed or times out.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→from typing import Any
    11→
    12→from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import ZoneRule
    13→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import LangConfig
    14→from .capabilities import (
    15→    SHARED_PHASE_LABELS,
    16→    capability_report,
    17→    generic_zone_rules,
    18→    make_file_finder,
    19→)
    20→from desloppify.languages._framework.generic_parts.parsers import (
    21→    PARSERS as _PARSERS,
    22→)
    23→from desloppify.languages._framework.generic_parts.parsers import (
    24→    parse_cargo,
    25→    parse_eslint,
    26→    parse_gnu,
    27→    parse_golangci,
    28→    parse_json,
    29→    parse_rubocop,
    30→)
    31→from desloppify.languages._framework.generic_parts.tool_factories import (
    32→    make_detect_fn,
    33→    make_tool_phase,
    34→)
    35→from desloppify.languages._framework.generic_parts.tool_spec import (
    36→    normalize_tool_specs,
    37→)
    38→from .registration import (
    39→    GenericLangOptions,
    40→    _build_generic_phases,
    41→    _register_generic_tool_specs,
    42→    _resolve_generic_extractors,
    43→)
    44→
    45→
    46→def generic_lang(
    47→    name: str,
    48→    extensions: list[str],
    49→    tools: list[dict[str, Any]],
    50→    *,
    51→    options: GenericLangOptions | None = None,
    52→    exclude: list[str] | None = None,
    53→    depth: str = "shallow",
    54→    detect_markers: list[str] | None = None,
    55→    default_src: str = ".",
    56→    treesitter_spec=None,
    57→    zone_rules: list[ZoneRule] | None = None,
    58→    test_coverage_module: Any | None = None,
    59→    entry_patterns: list[str] | None = None,
    60→) -> LangConfig:
    61→    """Build and register a generic language plugin from tool specs.
    62→
    63→    Each entry in `tools` is::
    64→
    65→        {"label": str, "cmd": str, "fmt": str, "id": str, "tier": int,
    66→         "fix_cmd": str | None}
    67→
    68→    When ``treesitter_spec`` is provided and ``tree-sitter-language-pack`` is
    69→    installed, the plugin gains function extraction (enables duplicate
    70→    detection), and optionally import analysis (enables coupling/orphan/cycle
    71→    detection and test-coverage analysis) for no additional configuration.
    72→
    73→    Returns the built LangConfig (also registered in the language registry).
    74→    """
    75→    opts = options or GenericLangOptions(
    76→        exclude=exclude,
    77→        depth=depth,
    78→        detect_markers=detect_markers,
    79→        default_src=default_src,
    80→        treesitter_spec=treesitter_spec,
    81→        zone_rules=zone_rules,
    82→        test_coverage_module=test_coverage_module,
    83→        entry_patterns=entry_patterns,
    84→    )
    85→
    86→    from desloppify.languages import register_generic_lang
    87→
    88→    tool_specs = normalize_tool_specs(tools, supported_formats=set(_PARSERS))
    89→    fixers = _register_generic_tool_specs(tool_specs)
    90→    file_finder, extract_fn, dep_graph_fn, has_treesitter, ts_spec = _resolve_generic_extractors(
    91→        path_extensions=extensions,
    92→        opts=opts,
    93→    )
    94→    phases = _build_generic_phases(
    95→        tool_specs=tool_specs,
    96→        ts_spec=ts_spec,
    97→        has_treesitter=has_treesitter,
    98→        extract_fn=extract_fn,
    99→        dep_graph_fn=dep_graph_fn,
   100→    )
   101→
   102→    cfg = LangConfig(
   103→        name=name,
   104→        extensions=extensions,
   105→        exclusions=opts.exclude or [],
   106→        default_src=opts.default_src,
   107→        build_dep_graph=dep_graph_fn,
   108→        entry_patterns=opts.entry_patterns or [],
   109→        barrel_names=set(),
   110→        phases=phases,
   111→        fixers=fixers,
   112→        get_area=None,
   113→        detect_commands={
   114→            tool["id"]: make_detect_fn(tool["cmd"], _PARSERS[tool["fmt"]])
   115→            for tool in tool_specs
   116→        },
   117→        extract_functions=extract_fn,
   118→        boundaries=[],
   119→        typecheck_cmd="",
   120→        file_finder=file_finder,
   121→        large_threshold=500,
   122→        complexity_threshold=15,
   123→        default_scan_profile="objective",
   124→        detect_markers=opts.detect_markers or [],
   125→        external_test_dirs=["tests", "test"],
   126→        test_file_extensions=extensions,
   127→        zone_rules=opts.zone_rules if opts.zone_rules is not None else generic_zone_rules(extensions),
   128→    )
   129→
   130→    # Set integration depth — upgrade when tree-sitter provides capabilities.
   131→    if has_treesitter and opts.depth in ("shallow", "minimal"):
   132→        cfg.integration_depth = "standard"
   133→    else:
   134→        cfg.integration_depth = opts.depth
   135→
   136→    # Register language-specific test coverage hooks if provided.
   137→    if opts.test_coverage_module is not None:
   138→        from desloppify.languages._framework.registry.state import register_lang_hooks
   139→
   140→        register_lang_hooks(name, test_coverage=opts.test_coverage_module)
   141→
   142→    register_generic_lang(name, cfg)
   143→    return cfg
   144→
   145→
   146→__all__ = [
   147→    "GenericLangOptions",
   148→    "SHARED_PHASE_LABELS",
   149→    "capability_report",
   150→    "generic_lang",
